# Inventory of features from the tests

Every test of the core's suite, of the web application's unit tests and of the editor's browser tests on 2026-11-16: 2,701 tests in 69 files. Each line is a test's name, a sentence that says what is true when it passes; together they say what the studio does and that it works. Each feature of [features.md](features.md) names the tests that prove it. Generated by `node scripts/inventory.mjs` from a complete run of the core's tests and the list of the browser tests ([ADR 0005](adr/0005-documentation-and-feature-inventory.md)); do not edit by hand.

Every unit test ran and passed in the run that wrote this file, the ZPL printed by Labelary included (`LABELARY=1`), but the documents' own test (`docs`), which checks this file and runs right after it is written. The browser tests are listed by Playwright and run by CI against the built application, on a desktop, a phone and a tablet held horizontally (a test about one layout is skipped in the others).

<details><summary><code>logistic</code> — 13 tests (<code>packages/core/test/logistic.test.ts</code>)</summary>

- the GS1 Logistic Label › is the page's size, its SSCC alone in the lowest symbol, the other element strings above it
- the GS1 Logistic Label › wraps From and To between words, or after a hyphen, slash or comma, within their halves of the label, at 3.5 mm while the label has room
- the GS1 Logistic Label › takes the free text down to 3 mm (6.6.4.4) when its lines leave the label too short, and then names it among the cures
- the GS1 Logistic Label › reads back every symbol from the SVG and the PDF
- the GS1 Logistic Label › refuses data without an SSCC, a symbol wider than the page, and more than the page holds
- the GS1 Logistic Label › fits the SSCC's content on a 4 × 6 in label: 2 mm margins, and as near 7 mm as they allow
- the GS1 Logistic Label › places a gs1-datamatrix to the right of the middle block, read back with the GS1-128
- the GS1 Logistic Label › places a dl-qrcode to the right of the middle block, read back with the GS1-128
- the GS1 Logistic Label › places a dl-datamatrix to the right of the middle block, read back with the GS1-128
- the GS1 Logistic Label › adds a second language under each data title and under FROM and TO, the contents on a row level
- the GS1 Logistic Label › shows a percent-encoded name or address in the middle block as people read it, as the code carries it under the symbols
- the GS1 Logistic Label › names a data title in Portuguese, Spanish, French, German and Italian, an AI of its own where one title has two meanings
- the GS1 Logistic Label › puts a line too wide for the room beside the 2D symbol below it, and refuses a page too short for both

</details>

- a picture › is drawn as rectangles that cover its dark pixels and nothing else, a run the row above has growing down
- a picture › is dithered by error diffusion: each grey as dots as many as its darkness, black and white as they are
- a picture › is dithered from its grey levels at its darkness, which darkens or lightens the greys and keeps white and black
- a picture › is dithered again at a printer's dots when dithered, where each dot under its centre would print it wrong

- generating every row › puts crop marks around each label's PDF and EPS and every page of the PDF of a label a page, not on label sheets
- generating every row › puts every label on the sheet chosen, read back

- which AI a column holds, from its header › leaves out what it does not know, and a second column for an AI already taken
- cells as the AI takes them › writes dates as YYMMDD from Excel's ISO form and from day-first forms; leaves other values as they are
- cells as the AI takes them › keeps a time after a YYMMDD date as HHMM when the AI carries one, its seconds when the AI takes them
- cells as the AI takes them › reads a time on a 12-hour clock with AM or PM: 12 AM is 00, 12 PM is 12
- cells as the AI takes them › reads (7007)'s harvest period as two dates, YYMMDDYYMMDD, and its first date alone as YYMMDD
- cells as the AI takes them › writes a date of birth as YYYYMMDD, its century never guessed, and (7251)'s time as HHMM
- cells as the AI takes them › writes a name or an address whose AI is percent-encoded with its spaces as + and accents as %XX, once
- cells as the AI takes them › reads a person's sex from a word in each of the six languages as ISO/IEC 5218's digit, not from a letter
- a row's cells by their column's header › keeps each cell trimmed under its header, leaves out a column without one and keeps the first of two
- a row's cells by their column's header › offers as fields the columns holding no AI, by a header a field can name, the first of two alike
- rows as identifications › takes the key, its qualifiers and the attributes, with the fixed values last
- rows as identifications › says why a row has no key
- rows as identifications › checks every row of a workbook with the Syntax Engine, numbering rows as the spreadsheet does
- rows as identifications › keeps the time of a workbook's date cells for the AIs that carry one, as each cell's format shows it
- rows as identifications › checks a patient list: GSRN, date of birth, names with accents and spaces, each row accepted by the Syntax Engine
- rows as identifications › takes the URI's own parameters from the columns that hold them, each named by its header
- rows as identifications › refuses a parameter whose header is made of digits, has no name or is given twice, at the parameters' columns
- rows as identifications › checks pasted rows the same way, a date the engine refuses reported for its row

</details>

- +AIDC data after the EPC › writes (02) 99999999999999 after an SGTIN+ and reads it back
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (17) 271231: 0001 0111 \| 0011011 1100 11111 — 6-digit date (14.5.8): YY, MM, DD in 7, 4 and 5 bits
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (10) ABC123: 0001 0000 \| 001 00110 1010 1011 1100 0001 0010 0011 — variable-length (14.5.6), upper case hexadecimal the shortest
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (3103) 000500: 0011 0001 0000 0011 \| 00000000000111110100 — fixed-bit-length (14.5.2): 6 digits in 20 bits
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (4330) 001234-: 0100 0011 0011 0000 \| 00000000010011010010 1 — a temperature and its minus sign (14.5.14)
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (7258) 1/3: 0111 0010 0101 1000 \| 0001 0011 — sequence indicator (14.5.15)
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (4307) BR: 0100 0011 0000 0111 \| 000001 010001 — country code (14.5.12)
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (8008) 2712312359: 1000 0000 0000 1000 \| 01 0011011 1100 11111 10111 111011 — variable-precision date+time (14.5.11), to the minute
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (7007) 271201271231: 0111 0000 0000 0111 \| 1 0011011 1100 00001 0011011 1100 11111 — a date range (14.5.10)
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (7007) 271231: 0111 0000 0000 0111 \| 0 0011011 1100 11111 — a date range's single date (14.5.10)
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (4321) 1: 0100 0011 0010 0001 \| 1 — single data bit (14.5.7)
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (30) 12: 0011 0000 \| 0010 0001100 — a 2-digit AI table K leaves out; numeric without indicator (14.5.13)
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (8004) 123AB: 1000 0000 0000 0100 \| 0001 0010 0011 1110 001 00010 1010 1011 — delimited numeric (14.5.5), its rest in upper case hexadecimal
- +AIDC data after the EPC › (8004) 12ab\_: 1000 0000 0000 0100 \| 0001 0010 1110 011 00011 011010 011011 111111 — delimited numeric, its rest in base 64
- +AIDC data after the EPC › writes several in their order, its qualifiers in the URI's path and the rest after '?', on a '++' too
- +AIDC data after the EPC › refuses an AI table F has not, a value its method cannot take, and an AI the EPC already carries

</details>

<details><summary><code>ndef</code> — 7 tests (<code>packages/core/test/ndef.test.ts</code>)</summary>

- an NDEF URI record › abbreviates https:// as code 4 in a short record: D1 01 length 55 04 and the rest
- an NDEF URI record › takes the longest prefix (https://www. over https://), urn:epc:id: for an EPC, none for an unknown scheme
- an NDEF URI record › writes a long record with a four-byte length, and reads one with an ID
- an NDEF message's records (the Check Studio, I3) › lists every record in its order, and finds the first URI record wherever it is
- an NDEF message's records (the Check Studio, I3) › stops at the record marked last, and refuses a message cut inside a record
- an NDEF message made of records (the Check Studio's NFC reading, 7.1 h) › gives the same bytes as a URI record written alone
- an NDEF message made of records (the Check Studio's NFC reading, 7.1 h) › marks the first record MB and the last ME, short records under 256 bytes, and reads back as given

</details>

<details><summary><code>sign</code> — 8 tests (<code>packages/core/test/sign.test.ts</code>)</summary>

- the payload › is figure 4-7's JSON-LD: the GS1 Digital Link URI as id, the GTIN, serial, batch, expiry as xsd:date, names, marking
- the payload › dates YYMMDD as section 7.12 chooses the century (49 years back, 50 ahead); day 00 is the month's last
- the payload › checks a marking's code against its regular expression
- signing › writes a compact JWS with ES256 and the key's thumbprint as kid, which Node verifies too
- signing › refuses a payload changed after signing, and another key
- signing › workflow 3: with the code taken out, verifies only once the code read from the marking is put back
- signing › takes back a key the person saved, with the same kid; refuses what is not a private P-256 key
- GS1 verifiable credentials (data model 1.2.1) › writes the key and product data credentials as its examples 5.5.1 and 5.6.1 do, secured as JWTs

</details>

## The Check Studio: a code read to be checked

<details><summary><code>camera</code> — 8 tests (<code>apps/web/test/camera.test.ts</code>)</summary>

- the camera's problems › name a refused, missing or busy camera from the error's name
- the track's controls › offer a torch and a zoom only where the capabilities say so
- the same reading within three seconds › counts once; again after the interval; another symbol at once
- the same reading within three seconds › tells symbols apart by their identifier and bytes
- the readings of a frame or a picture › keep the decoder's bytes, identifier, corners and the picture's size
- the readings of a frame or a picture › say when a linear symbol read by the camera or in a picture may have a Composite part left out
- the readings of a frame or a picture › name each symbol as a person knows it

- a computer shows every step at once, without the destinations' bar

- the version under the name, the same as About's, links to About
